Note for v2:
The quantum spectral curve for AdS
{}_{3}\times
S
{}^{3}\times
T
4
proposed independently in
Cavaglia:2021eqr
agrees with our proposal. There are substantial additions in v2 of our paper, but not in sections where overlap with
Cavaglia:2021eqr
exists: there we did not add new results but only corrected typos and improved clarity of explanations.

Appendix A
Monodromy without Hodge duality

In this appendix we fill in details for the monodromy bootstrap of type A, when the properly defined analytic continuation around the branch point leads at most to a continuous symmetry transformation of the physical system. We parameterise LHPA Q-system
\mathcal{Q}_{A|I}^{\downarrow}
simply in the same way as we parameterise
\mathcal{Q}_{A|I}
:

\displaystyle{\bf P}_{a}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathcal{Q}_{a|\emptyset}\,,

\displaystyle{\bf P}^{a}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathcal{Q}^{a|\emptyset}\,,

\displaystyle{\bf Q}_{i}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathcal{Q}_{\emptyset|i}

\displaystyle{\bf Q}^{i}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathcal{Q}^{\emptyset|i}\,,

(141a)

\displaystyle{\bf P}_{a}^{\downarrow}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathcal{Q}_{a|\emptyset}^{\downarrow}\,,

\displaystyle{\bf P}_{\downarrow}^{a}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathcal{Q}_{\downarrow}^{a|\emptyset}\,,

\displaystyle{\bf Q}_{i}^{\downarrow}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathcal{Q}_{\emptyset|i}^{\downarrow}

\displaystyle{\bf Q}^{i}_{\downarrow}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathcal{Q}^{\emptyset|i}_{\downarrow}\,.

(141b)

Hasse diagram is still given by (
21
), and the LHPA Q-system is described by exactly the same diagram but with all objects being sub- or super-scripted with
\downarrow
.

Rotation in the physical kinematics is required to act on bosonic indices only. Together with gauge transformations, we have
{\bf P}_{a}^{\downarrow}={\check{\mathsf{h}}}\,\mu_{a}{}^{b}{\bf P}_{b}
. Rotation in the mirror kinematics is required to act on fermionic indices only, and we introduce it by
{\bf Q}_{i}^{\downarrow}={\hat{\mathsf{h}}}^{-1}\omega_{i}{}^{j}{\bf Q}_{j}
. Denote
\det\limits_{1\leq a,b\leq 2}\mu_{a}{}^{b}=\mu^{2}
,
\det\limits_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\omega_{i}{}^{j}=\omega^{2}
. Then it follows

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{Q}_{12|12}^{\downarrow}}{\mathcal{Q}_{12|12}}=\frac{g^{2}}{g_{\downarrow}^{2}}=\left(\omega^{+}\right)^{2}=\left(\mu^{+}\right)^{2}\,,\ \ \ \ -1/2<\Im(u)<1/2\,.

(142)

Consider
\mu(u)
and
\omega(u)
for
u
slightly above real axis. According to (
142
), these two functions are equal. Now, if we shift the argument by
\mathtt{i}
up in the mirror kinematics, the value of
\mu^{2}=\omega^{2}
won’t change due to the periodicity of
\mu^{2}
. And, similarly, it won’t change if we shift the argument by
\mathtt{i}
in the physical kinematics where
\omega^{2}
is periodic. Now it is not difficult to deduce that
\mu^{2}=\omega^{2}
are just
\mathtt{i}
-periodic functions without any cuts. By an appropriate H-rotation of Q
↓
-system, we can set
\mu^{2}=\omega^{2}=1
, and we choose the sign of square root to get
\mu=\omega=1
. Then we also have
g^{2}=g_{\downarrow}^{2}
and therefore
\mathcal{Q}_{12|12}=\mathcal{Q}_{12|12}^{\downarrow}=\frac{1}{g^{2}}
.

Recall the important invariant combination

\displaystyle{\bf Q}^{i}\,{\bf Q}_{i}={\bf P}^{a}\,{\bf P}_{a}=\frac{1}{F}-F\,,\quad F=\frac{g^{+}}{g^{-}}\,.

(143)

We see that, unlike in the type-B scenario,
F
is simply analytic everywhere.

With the choice to normalise
\mu=\omega=1
, we can write the full set of relations between
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\downarrow}
:

Mirror

\displaystyle{\bf P}_{a}^{\downarrow}

\displaystyle={\check{\mathsf{h}}}\,\mu_{a}{}^{b}\,{\bf P}_{b}

(144a)

\displaystyle{\bf Q}_{i}^{\downarrow}

\displaystyle={\check{\mathsf{h}}}^{-1}\,{\bf Q}_{i}

(144b)

\displaystyle{\bf P}_{\downarrow}^{a}

\displaystyle={\check{\mathsf{h}}}^{-1}{\bf P}^{b}\,(\mu^{-1})_{b}{}^{a}

(144c)

\displaystyle{\bf Q}_{\downarrow}^{i}

\displaystyle={\check{\mathsf{h}}}\,{\bf Q}^{i}

(144d)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{a|i}^{\downarrow}

\displaystyle=(\mu_{a}{}^{b})^{+}\,\mathcal{Q}_{b|i}

(144e)

Physical

\displaystyle{\bf P}_{a}^{\downarrow}

\displaystyle={\hat{\mathsf{h}}}\,{\bf P}_{a}

(145a)

\displaystyle{\bf Q}_{i}^{\downarrow}

\displaystyle={{\hat{\mathsf{h}}}}^{-1}\,\omega_{i}{}^{j}\,{\bf Q}_{j}

(145b)

\displaystyle{\bf P}_{\downarrow}^{a}

\displaystyle={{\hat{\mathsf{h}}}}^{-1}\,{\bf P}^{a}

(145c)

\displaystyle{\bf Q}_{\downarrow}^{i}

\displaystyle={\hat{\mathsf{h}}}\,{\bf Q}^{j}(\omega^{-1})_{j}{}^{i}

(145d)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{a|i}^{\downarrow}

\displaystyle=(\omega_{i}{}^{j})^{+}\,\mathcal{Q}_{a|j}

(145e)

We derive further consequences in full analogy with the type-B case.

With

\displaystyle r\equiv\frac{{\check{\mathsf{h}}}}{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\hat{\mathsf{h}}}}\vphantom{{\hat{\mathsf{h}}}}}\,,

(146)

the monodromy properties of
{\bf P}
and
{\bf Q}
are

Clock-wise

\displaystyle\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf P}}\vphantom{{\bf P}}_{a}

\displaystyle=r\,\mu_{a}{}^{b}\,{\bf P}_{b}\,,

(147a)

\displaystyle\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf P}}\vphantom{{\bf P}}^{a}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r}\,{\bf P}^{b}(\mu^{-1})_{b}{}^{a}\,,\

(147b)

\displaystyle\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf Q}}\vphantom{{\bf Q}}_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r}\,\omega_{i}{}^{j}\,{\bf Q}_{j}\,,

(147c)

\displaystyle\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf Q}}\vphantom{{\bf Q}}^{i}

\displaystyle={r}\,{\bf Q}^{j}\,\left(\omega^{-1}\right)_{j}{}^{i}\,,

(147d)

Counterclock-wise

\displaystyle\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf P}}\vphantom{{\bf P}}_{a}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{r}\vphantom{r}}\left(\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}^{-1}\right)_{a}{}^{b}\,{\bf P}_{b}\,,

(148a)

\displaystyle\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf P}}\vphantom{{\bf P}}^{a}

\displaystyle=\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{r}\vphantom{r}\,{\bf P}^{b}\,\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}_{b}{}^{a}\,,

(148b)

\displaystyle\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf Q}}\vphantom{{\bf Q}}_{i}

\displaystyle=\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{r}\vphantom{r}\left(\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\omega}\vphantom{\omega}^{-1}\right)_{i}{}^{j}\,{\bf Q}_{j}\,,

(148c)

\displaystyle\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf Q}}\vphantom{{\bf Q}}^{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{r}\vphantom{r}}\,{\bf Q}^{j}\,\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\omega}\vphantom{\omega}_{j}{}^{i}\,.

(148d)

From comparison of equations (
144e
) and (
145e
) for
\mathcal{Q}_{a|i}^{\downarrow}
we get

\displaystyle\omega_{i}{}^{j}

\displaystyle=-\mu_{a}{}^{b}\,\left(\mathcal{Q}_{b|i}\mathcal{Q}^{a|j}\right)^{-}\,,

\displaystyle\left(\omega^{-1}\right)_{i}{}^{j}

\displaystyle=-\left(\mu^{-1}\right)_{a}{}^{b}\,\left(\mathcal{Q}_{b|i}\,\mathcal{Q}^{a|j}\right)^{-}\,,

\displaystyle 0<\Im(u)<1\,.

(149)

Then it follows

\displaystyle{\bf P}^{a}\mu_{a}{}^{b}{\bf P}_{b}

\displaystyle={\bf P}^{a}\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}_{a}{}^{b}{\bf P}_{b}

and hence

\displaystyle{\bf P}^{a}\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf P}}\vphantom{{\bf P}}_{a}=\frac{r}{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{r}\vphantom{r}}\,\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf P}}\vphantom{{\bf P}}^{a}{\bf P}_{a}\,,

(150a)

\displaystyle{\bf P}^{a}(\mu^{-1})_{a}{}^{b}{\bf P}_{b}

\displaystyle={\bf P}^{a}(\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}^{-1})_{a}{}^{b}{\bf P}_{b}

and hence

\displaystyle{\bf P}^{a}\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf P}}\vphantom{{\bf P}}_{a}=\frac{r}{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{r}\vphantom{r}}\,\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf P}}\vphantom{{\bf P}}^{a}{\bf P}_{a}\,.

(150b)

From
0=\Delta(\mathcal{Q}_{a|i}^{\downarrow\,-})=\Delta(\mu_{a}{}^{b}\mathcal{Q}_{b|i}^{-})
one gets

\displaystyle\mu_{a}{}^{b}-\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}_{a}{}^{b}

\displaystyle=\frac{F}{r}\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf P}}\vphantom{{\bf P}}_{a}{\bf P}^{b}-\frac{F}{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{r}\vphantom{r}}{\bf P}_{a}\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf P}}\vphantom{{\bf P}}^{b}=F\left(\mu_{a}{}^{c}{\bf P}_{c}{\bf P}^{b}-{\bf P}_{a}{\bf P}^{c}\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}_{c}{}^{b}\right)\,,

(151a)

\displaystyle\mu_{a}{}^{b}-\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}_{a}{}^{b}

\displaystyle=\frac{F}{r}\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf P}}\vphantom{{\bf P}}_{a}{\bf P}^{b}-\frac{F}{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{r}\vphantom{r}}\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf P}}\vphantom{{\bf P}}}\vphantom{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf P}}\vphantom{{\bf P}}}_{a}\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf P}}\vphantom{{\bf P}}^{b}=F\left(\mu_{a}{}^{c}{\bf P}_{c}{\bf P}^{b}-\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}_{a}{}^{c}\mu_{c}{}^{d}{\bf P}_{d}{\bf P}^{e}(\mu^{-1})_{e}{}^{b}\right)\,.

(151b)

Appendix B
Proof of no-go theorems

In this appendix we prove the no-go theorems that restrict considerably or forbid completely the possibility of branch points of square root type. By being a branch point of this type we mean the property
\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{f}\vphantom{f}=\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{f}\vphantom{f}
. We shall use mostly tensor notation of Section
5
. Hence
\mu
shall denote a matrix not a square root of its determinant.

We shall not study case D because fourth-order monodromies are more natural to be asked about for it. From the remaining cases, it is in principle enough to consider case C. Indeed, A is reproduced for
\bar{Q}^{*}=Q
and B is reproduced for
\bar{Q}=Q
. However A and B have simpler proofs that use extra symmetries, while case C is considerably more technical. We therefore decided to outline the proofs for the three cases separately.

Two assumptions are being used: algebraic independence of
{\bf P}
-functions in the sense explained below, and also the following one: if a function
R
is free from Zhukovsky branch points then
\sqrt{R}
is free from them as well, this shall be referred to as
\sqrt{\phantom{a}}
-assumption. A supportive observation for it is that appearance of such branch points is only possible if
R
has a pole or a zero at this very specific non-dynamical position. Moreover, the assumption will be only used when
R
is periodic in which case it means the presence of infinite ladders of such poles/zeros to violate
\sqrt{\phantom{a}}
-assumption. In fact, we already used this type of assumption by claiming that
F
has only one branch cut: only the ratio
\left(g^{+}/g^{-}\right)^{2}
is guaranteed to be UHPA from the postulated analytic properties of Q-functions, while we relied on analyticity of
g^{+}/g^{-}=\sqrt{\left(g^{+}/g^{-}\right)^{2}}
in the arguments.

Case B

Equation (
51
) in tensor notations reads

\displaystyle\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}=\mu\,\left(1+\frac{1}{F}{\bf P}^{*}\otimes{\bf P}\right)\,\left(1+\frac{1}{F}\mu\,{\bf P}^{*}\otimes{\bf P}\,\mu^{-1}\right)^{\rm T}\,.

(152)

We remind that
1-F{\bf P}^{*}\otimes{\bf P}
is the inverse of
1+\frac{1}{F}{\bf P}^{*}\otimes{\bf P}
, this guarantees that these matrices are invertible. We also shall use that
\mu
is invertible as it realises an H-symmetry.

It is handy to define
{\mathcal{A}}=\mu\left(1+\frac{1}{F}{\bf P}^{*}\otimes{\bf P}\right)
. Then, using (
152
) and (
66b
), equation
\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}=\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}
becomes after matrix rearrangements

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{\rm T}\,\mu^{-1}\,{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{A}}\,\mu^{-1}\,{\mathcal{A}}^{\rm T}\,.

(153)

Let us decompose
{\mathcal{A}}
into symmetric and antisymmetric parts
{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{A}}_{+}+{\mathcal{A}}_{-}
. Then one gets
{\mathcal{A}}_{+}\,\mu^{-1}\,{\mathcal{A}}_{-}={\mathcal{A}}_{-}\,\mu^{-1}\,{\mathcal{A}}_{+}
and, by adding the tautological
{\mathcal{A}}_{-}\,\mu^{-1}\,{\mathcal{A}}_{-}={\mathcal{A}}_{-}\,\mu^{-1}\,{\mathcal{A}}_{-}
, we conclude

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}\,\mu^{-1}\,{\mathcal{A}}_{-}={\mathcal{A}}_{-}\,\mu^{-1}\,{\mathcal{A}}\,.

(154)

Now two-dimensionality of the problem is going to be used. Define
\epsilon=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{0}&{1}\\
{-1}&{0}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. Any
2\times 2
anti-symmetric matrix is proportional to
\epsilon
which implies, if
{\mathcal{A}}_{-}\neq 0
,
{\mathcal{A}}\,\mu^{-1}\,\epsilon=\epsilon\,\mu^{-1}\,{\mathcal{A}}
. Recall now the definition of
{\mathcal{A}}
to simplify the equation to the explicit linear in
\mu
equality
\mu\,{\bf P}^{*}\otimes{\bf P}=-\epsilon\,{\bf P}^{*}\otimes{\bf P}\,\epsilon\,\mu
. Contracting from the left with
{\bf P}^{*}
, the r.h.s. vanishes leaving us with

\displaystyle{\bf P}^{*}\,\mu\,{\bf P}^{*}=0

(155)

which is (
63
) implying that
\mu
is antisymmetric.

Let us assume now that
{\bf P}^{*}\,\mu\,{\bf P}^{*}\neq 0
but instead
{\mathcal{A}}_{-}=0
. Explicitly,
{\mathcal{A}}_{-}=0
reads

\displaystyle\left(F\epsilon^{ba}+{\bf P}^{b}{\bf P}_{c}\epsilon^{ca}\right)^{[2n]}\mu_{ab}=0\,,\quad n=0,1,2,\ldots\,,

(156)

where we added the shifts by
n
using that
\mu
is periodic in the mirror kinematics. Introduce single-index parameterisation
\mu_{1}\equiv\mu_{11},\mu_{2}\equiv\mu_{12},\mu_{3}\equiv\mu_{21},\mu_{3}\equiv\mu_{22}
. Then the last equation can be understood as the matrix equation
M_{n\alpha}\mu_{\alpha}=0
, where
M
is the matrix with semi-infinite number of rows.

Prior to continue the main analysis, we need to show that the kernel of
M
can be spanned by periodic in
u
functions without branch points, this is a rather general statement not specific to the details of the problem. Indeed, let
r<4
is the rank of
M
. Then there exist a
(r+1)\times(r+1)
submatrix of
M
with
n=0,\ldots,r
and some subset of
\alpha
’s that has precisely one zero mode. For definiteness, let this subset is
\alpha=1,\ldots,r+1

32
32

32

If not, we can use re-labelling or even make a linear transformation on
\mu_{\alpha}
, so no loss of generality for this choice.
. Denote the zero mode by
c_{\alpha}^{(1)}
. It is clear that
\sum\limits_{\alpha=1}^{r+1}M_{n+1,\alpha}\left(c_{\alpha}^{(1)}\right)^{[2]}=0
and hence
\sum\limits_{\alpha=1}^{r+1}M_{n\alpha}\left(c_{\alpha}^{(1)}\right)^{[2]}=0
for any
n
, therefore
\left(c_{\alpha}^{(1)}\right)^{[2]}
is a zero mode, but because there is only one zero mode of the
(r+1)\times(r+1)
submatrix, it must be
\left(c_{\alpha}^{(1)}\right)^{[2]}=\Lambda(u)\,c_{\alpha}^{(1)}
. We can normalise at will and set
e.g.
c_{r+1}^{(1)}=1
(if
c_{r+1}^{(1)}=0
, pick any other non-zero coefficient) getting the periodicity property
\left(c_{\alpha}^{(1)}\right)^{[2]}=c_{\alpha}^{(1)}
. Now, recall that any eigenvector of a matrix can be represented, up to a normalisation, as a rational combination of matrix entries and the eigenvalue. Because
M_{n\alpha}
depends only on
{\bf P}
and
F
who are UHPA, we conclude that
c_{\alpha}^{(1)}
in the chosen normalisation is also UHPA and, by its periodicity, is free from cuts everywhere.

If
r<3
, pick
(r+2)\times(r+2)
submatrix of
M
with two zero modes. One zero mode shall be
c_{\alpha}^{(1)}
with
c_{r+2}^{(1)}=0
and another shall be denoted as
\bar{c}_{\alpha}
, we normalise it to have
\bar{c}_{r+2}=1
(this coefficient cannot be zero because
\bar{c}
is linearly independent from
c^{(1)}
). The most general periodicity property with this normalisation is
{\bar{c}}^{[2]}=\bar{c}+\Lambda(u)\,c^{(1)}
. Notice now that
\Lambda(u)={\bar{c}}_{r+1}^{[2]}-{\bar{c}}_{r+1}
, hence we can introduce
c^{(2)}:=\bar{c}-{\bar{c}}_{r+1}c^{(1)}
, this vector is a zero mode of
M
which is linearly independent from
c^{(1)}
and is periodic in
u
. By the same argument as above, it is free from cuts.

If needed, we can keep reasoning in the same manner and construct linearly independent
c^{(i)}
which span the kernel of
M
and are free from branch points. One then has
\mu_{\alpha}=\sum\limits_{i}\Lambda_{i}(u)c_{\alpha}^{(i)}
.

If
\mu_{ab}
is a symmetric matrix or if
M
has more than one zero modes, there must exist a zero mode
c
such that
c_{ab}
is a symmetric matrix which therefore satisfies

\displaystyle\left({\bf P}^{b}{\bf P}_{c}\epsilon^{ca}\right)^{[2n]}c_{ab}=0\,.

(157)

Introduce
v=({\bf P}_{1}{\bf P}^{1}-{\bf P}_{2}{\bf P}^{2},{\bf P}_{2}{\bf P}^{1},{\bf P}_{1}{\bf P}^{2})
. The last equation has a non-trivial solution only if
v^{[2n]}\wedge v^{[2m]}\wedge v^{[2k]}=0
for any
n,m,k\in\mathbb{Z}
. Our assumption on algebraic independence on
{\bf P}
is that this property cannot be fulfilled. For instance, if Q-functions have power-like large-
u
behaviour and
{\bf P}_{a}\sim u^{-\lambda_{a}}
then
{\bf P}^{a}\sim u^{\lambda_{a}+{\rm const}}
which, for
\lambda_{1}\neq\lambda_{2}
, is already enough to verify the assumed algebraic independence.

We therefore end up with the case when there is exactly one zero mode of
M
and such that
\mu_{ab}=\Lambda c_{ab}
is a matrix which has both non-zero symmetric and antisymmetric parts. The discontinuity properties of
\mu_{ab}
are now concentrated in the scalar function
\Lambda
. Using (
58
), we see
\Lambda/\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\Lambda}\vphantom{\Lambda}=F^{2}
and then (
60
) implies
F^{2}=1
. But this immediately tells us that
\Lambda
and hence
\mu_{ab}
have no branch points on the real axis. By periodicity,
\mu_{ab}
is then free from any branch points.

In conclusion, (
156
) cannot be satisfied by
\mu_{ab}
with branch points and therefore the only non-trivial option to satisfy
\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}_{ab}=\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}_{ab}
is
\mu_{ab}\propto\epsilon_{ab}
.

Case A

First let us comment on why
\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf P}}\vphantom{{\bf P}}\otimes\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf P}}\vphantom{{\bf P}}^{*}=\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf P}}\vphantom{{\bf P}}\otimes\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bf P}}\vphantom{{\bf P}}^{*}
implies branch points of square root type for
\mu
. The idea is to use relations (
150
) which, under this assumption on the cut structure of
{\bf P}\otimes{\bf P}^{*}
, imply
r/\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{r}\vphantom{r}=\pm 1
that in turn implies, using (
151
), equality of clock- and counter-clock-wise discontinuities
\Delta(\mu_{ab})
. A possible escape from this conclusion is
{\bf P}^{a}\mu_{a}{}^{b}{\bf P}_{b}=0
, but for this equation we shall assume algebraic independence of
{\bf P}
in the sense of the previous subsection. Then
({\bf P}^{a}{\bf P}_{b})^{[2n]}\mu_{a}{}^{b}=0
can have potentially only one zero mode which constraints
\mu
to the form
\mu_{a}{}^{b}=\Lambda(u)c_{a}{}^{b}
, where
c_{a}{}^{b}
is analytic. Then
\Lambda^{2}=\det{\mu}/\det{c}
is analytic and we use
\sqrt{\phantom{a}}
-assumption to state that
\Lambda(u)
is also analytic.

To prove the no-go theorem for square root cuts of
\mu
, let us introduce matrix
\Pi=1+\frac{1}{F}{\bf P}\otimes{\bf P}^{*}
. Using it, QSC equations become even more elegant

\displaystyle\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\Pi}\vphantom{\Pi}=\mu\,\Pi\,\mu^{-1}\,,\quad\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}=\Pi\,\mu\,\Pi^{-1}\,.

(158)

Relation (
151b
) can be re-written in this conventions as
\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}=\mu\,\Pi^{-1}\,\mu\,\Pi\,\mu^{-1}
, and then the square root cut assumption
\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}=\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}
leads to

\displaystyle(\mu\,\Pi^{-1})^{2}=(\Pi^{-1}\mu)^{2}\,.

(159)

Now we shall apply Hamilton-Cayley using the two-dimensionality of the problem: any
2\times 2
matrix satisfies
M^{2}-M\,\Tr M+\det M=0
. It simplifies the last equation to

\displaystyle[\mu,\Pi^{-1}]\Tr(\mu\,\Pi^{-1})=0\,.

(160)

Using the same assumptions on independence of
{\bf P}_{a},{\bf P}^{a}
as before, we get that
\Tr(\mu\,\Pi^{-1})=0
implies
\mu=\Lambda\,c
, we already explained why this leads to
\mu
without branch points. As for the possibility of
[\mu,\Pi^{-1}]=0
, we can readily use (
158
) to get
\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}=\mu
.

Case C

Using matrices
{\Pi}=1+\frac{1}{F}{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}\otimes{{{{\bf P}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{*}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
,
\bar{{\Pi}}=1+\frac{1}{\bar{F}}{{{\bar{{\bf P}}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}\otimes{{{\bar{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{*}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
, the QSC equations become

\displaystyle\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\Pi}}\vphantom{{\Pi}}

\displaystyle={{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}\,\bar{{\Pi}}^{-{\rm T}}\,{{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{-1}\,,

\displaystyle\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\bar{{\Pi}}}\vphantom{\bar{{\Pi}}}

\displaystyle={\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}\,{\Pi}^{-{\rm T}}\,{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{-1}\,

(161a)

\displaystyle{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}

\displaystyle=\bar{{\Pi}}\,{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}\,{\Pi}^{\rm T}\,,

\displaystyle{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bar{\mu}}}\vphantom{{\bar{\mu}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}

\displaystyle={\Pi}\,{{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}\,\bar{{\Pi}}^{\rm T}\,,

(161b)

where
M^{-{\rm T}}
means the inverse transpose of
M
.

Equivalence of square root cut assumptions.

An easy consequence of the above rewriting of QSC is

\displaystyle\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\Pi}}\vphantom{{\Pi}}\,{\Pi}^{-1}={{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}\,{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bar{\mu}}}\vphantom{{\bar{\mu}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{-1}\,.

(162)

From here it is clear that conditions
\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\Pi}}\vphantom{{\Pi}}=\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\Pi}}\vphantom{{\Pi}}
and
{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}={\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}
are equivalent (and the same holds for the pair
\bar{{\Pi}},{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}
). Property
{{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{{\bf P}}}\vphantom{{{\bf P}}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}={{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle*$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{{\bf P}}}\vphantom{{{\bf P}}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
is potentially a slightly stronger condition than that for
{\Pi}
. However, in the proof of this appendix, only relations of
{\bf P}\mu
-system shall be of relevance while analytic properties of
{\bf Q}
shall not be appealed to. For this reason we can pick a gauge where functions
{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}_{1},{{{\bar{{\bf P}}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}_{1}
have square root cuts (the choice
{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}_{1\vphantom{\dot{1}}}={{{\bar{{\bf P}}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}_{\dot{1}}=1
will do), in this gauge branch cut properties of
{\Pi}
and
{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}},{{{\bar{{\bf P}}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
are the same.

Non-vanishing of bilinear combinations.

As we already covered cases A,B, we will assume there is no particular algebraic relation between
{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
and
{{{\bar{{\bf P}}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
meaning that the combinations
{{{\bar{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{*}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}\mu{{{{\bf P}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{*}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}={{{\bar{{\bf P}}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{\dot{a}}\mu_{\dot{a}a}{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{a}\propto{{{\bar{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{*}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}{{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bar{{\bf P}}}}\vphantom{{\bar{{\bf P}}}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
and all other bilinears
{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}\mu{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}\propto{{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{{\bf P}}}\vphantom{{{\bf P}}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
(with all consistent arrangements of bars and stars in
{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}\mu{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
, and including inverses of
\mu
) shall be non-vanishing. The argument is of the same type as used for (
157
).

Case
F^{2}=1

This one is special because
{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}\otimes{{{{\bf P}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{*}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
becomes nilpotent in the presence of the zero charge constraint. By using
\sqrt{\phantom{a}}
-assumption and redefining
{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}},{{{\bar{{\bf P}}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
we can reach precisely all the normalisations stated for AdS
3
/CFT
2
conjecture, and hence we can use relations (
114
)
33
33

33

While reaching these normalisations, we perform rescaling
{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}\to\frac{1}{\sqrt{\det{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}}}{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}
. By
\sqrt{\phantom{a}}
-assumption,
\sqrt{\det{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}}
is analytic but it is potentially anti-periodic. Hence such a rescaling could create an anti-periodic
{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}
. This is not an issue for the current proof because periodicity of
{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}
is only used for arguments of type (
157
), these arguments work for
{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}
having any period and not only
\mathtt{i}
. The remark applies for similar rescalings in other parts of the proof.
. We also choose a gauge with
r=\bar{r}=1
. Then we conclude, from (
114a
) assuming square root branch cut, that
{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}+{{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{\rm T}=G
is an everywhere analytic function. If
G
is non-zero, it should satisfy
G\,{\Pi}^{-{\rm T}}=\bar{{\Pi}}\,G
which is impossible given algebraic independence of
{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
and
{{{\bar{{\bf P}}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
(note that we check a matrix equation and so algebraic relations
{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}_{a}{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{a}={{{\bar{{\bf P}}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}_{\dot{a}}{{{\bar{{\bf P}}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{\dot{a}}=0
are not enough to get non-vanishing
G
).

Then
{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}=-{{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{\rm T}
, from here there are many ways to show impossibility of square root branch points. For instance, we can use (
161a
) to deduce that
{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{-1}{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}
commutes with
\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\Pi}}\vphantom{{\Pi}}^{\rm T}
and then use (
162
) to decide that
[\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\Pi}}\vphantom{{\Pi}},{\Pi}]=0
. Then equality
{{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{{\bf P}}}\vphantom{{{\bf P}}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}\otimes{{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{{\bf P}}}\vphantom{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{*}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}=\Lambda\,{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}\otimes{{{{\bf P}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{*}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
for some
\Lambda
is implied, but it is impossible to satisfy it: contract from the left with
{{{{\bf P}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{*}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
, we agreed on
{{{{\bf P}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{*}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}{{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{{\bf P}}}\vphantom{{{\bf P}}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}\neq 0
while
{{{{\bf P}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{*}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}=0
.

Case
F^{2}\neq 1

Introduce the following notation

\displaystyle{\Pi}[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]=\lambda_{1}+(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})\frac{{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}\otimes{{{{\bf P}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{*}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}}{\frac{1}{F}-F}\,.

(163)

This is a matrix with eigenvalues
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}
and, respectively, eigenvectors
\epsilon{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{*}
,
{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
. Matrices
{\Pi}[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]
form a commutative family whose members are described faithfully by
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}
(faithfulness is specific to the two-dimensionality of the problem). In the co-moving frame with zweibein
\epsilon{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{*},{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
, they are diagonal. Note also that
{\Pi}[\lambda,\lambda]=\lambda\,1
,
i.e.
matrices
{\Pi}[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]
diagonalise at coinciding eigenvalues but not become of a Jordan block type, here condition
F^{2}\neq 1
is important. Finally, we notice that if the entries of
{\Pi}[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]
as a
2\times 2
matrix have branch points of square root type, so do
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}
due to linearity of (
163
).

Apart from obvious
{\Pi}[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]\,{\Pi}[\lambda_{1}^{\prime},\lambda_{2}^{\prime}]={\Pi}[\lambda_{1}\,\lambda_{1}^{\prime},\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{2}^{\prime}]
,
{\Pi}[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]+{\Pi}[\lambda_{1}^{\prime},\lambda_{2}^{\prime}]={\Pi}[\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{1}^{\prime},\lambda_{2}+\lambda_{2}^{\prime}]
, one has also simple behaviour under conjugation with
\epsilon=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle\epsilon\,{\Pi}[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]\,\epsilon^{-1}={\Pi}^{\rm T}[\lambda_{2},\lambda_{1}]\,.

(164)

Introduce also
\bar{{\Pi}}[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]=\lambda_{1}+(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})\frac{{{{\bar{{\bf P}}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}\otimes{{{\bar{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{*}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}}{\frac{1}{\bar{F}}-\bar{F}}
. We have in particular
{\Pi}={\Pi}[1,1/{F^{2}}]
,
\bar{{\Pi}}=\bar{{\Pi}}[1,1/{\bar{F}^{2}}]
, and equations (
161a
) can be generalised to

\displaystyle\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\Pi}}\vphantom{{\Pi}}[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]

\displaystyle={{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}\,\bar{{\Pi}}^{\rm T}[\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\lambda}\vphantom{\lambda}_{1},\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\lambda}\vphantom{\lambda}_{2}]\,{{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{-1}\,,

\displaystyle\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\bar{{\Pi}}}\vphantom{\bar{{\Pi}}}[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]

\displaystyle={\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}\,{\Pi}^{\rm T}[\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\lambda}\vphantom{\lambda}_{1},\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\lambda}\vphantom{\lambda}_{2}]\,{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{-1}\,.

(165)

Under square root cut hypothesis we can also write
\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\bar{{\Pi}}}\vphantom{\bar{{\Pi}}}[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]={\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bar{\mu}}}\vphantom{{\bar{\mu}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{{\rm T}}\,H^{\rm T}[\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\lambda}\vphantom{\lambda}_{1},\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\lambda}\vphantom{\lambda}_{2}]\,{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bar{\mu}}}\vphantom{{\bar{\mu}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{-\rm T}
. Equating to
\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\bar{{\Pi}}}\vphantom{\bar{{\Pi}}}
from (
165
), we see that the combination
{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{\rm T}{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bar{\mu}}}\vphantom{{\bar{\mu}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{-1}
commutes with matrices of type
{\Pi}[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]
and hence itself is a matrix of this type.

The ratio
\det{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}/\det{{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}
is an analytic function as follows from (
161b
). We rescale
\det{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}
to set the ratio to one. Then
\det({\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{\rm T}{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bar{\mu}}}\vphantom{{\bar{\mu}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{-1})=\det({\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{\rm T}{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\mu}\vphantom{\mu}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{-1})=F^{2}\bar{F}^{2}
. Hence

\displaystyle{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{\rm T}{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\bar{\mu}}}\vphantom{{\bar{\mu}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{-1}={\Pi}\left[\beta,\frac{F^{2}\bar{F}^{2}}{\beta}\right]

(166)

for some
\beta
(who has branch points of square root type, as all other functions). We then get

\displaystyle{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{\rm T}={\Pi}[\beta,\frac{\bar{F}^{2}}{\beta}]\,{{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}\,\bar{{\Pi}}^{\rm T}\,.

(167)

Another interesting combination is
{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{\rm T}{{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{-1}
whose analytic continuation is isospectral

\displaystyle\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{\rm T}{{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{-1}}\vphantom{{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{\rm T}{{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{-1}}={\Pi}\,{{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{\rm T}{{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{-1}}\,{\Pi}^{-1}

(168)

We substitute (
167
) to the right of this equation, and we substitute
{{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{\rm T}{{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{-1}}={\Pi}[\beta,\frac{\bar{F}^{2}}{\beta}]\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\Pi}}\vphantom{{\Pi}}^{-1}
to its left. After processing the obtained relation using (
161a
) and (
167
), equality (
168
) rearranges to

\displaystyle{\Pi}[\beta,\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{\bar{F}^{2}}{F^{2}}]={{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}\,\bar{{\Pi}}^{\rm T}[\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\beta}\vphantom{\beta},\frac{1}{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\beta}\vphantom{\beta}}\frac{\bar{F}^{2}}{F^{2}}]\,{{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{-1}=\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{\Pi}}\vphantom{{\Pi}}[\beta,\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{\bar{F}^{2}}{F^{2}}]\,.

(169)

Conjugation by
{{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}
is an isospectral operation, hence it must be that
\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\beta}\vphantom{\beta}=\beta
or
\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\beta}\vphantom{\beta}=\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{\bar{F}^{2}}{F^{2}}
. However, we can discard the second option: a check to do is to contract (
169
) with
{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}\epsilon
from the left and with
\epsilon{{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{{\bf P}}}\vphantom{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{*}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
from the right; using the agreement
{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}{{\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{{{\bf P}}}\vphantom{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{*}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}\neq 0
, we get directly
\beta=\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\beta}\vphantom{\beta}
.

Now that it is established that
\overset{\scalebox{0.8}{\raisebox{-1.99306pt}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{$\scriptstyle\hphantom{*}$}}\resizebox{0}{0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sim$}}}}{\beta}\vphantom{\beta}=\beta
, it is easy from (
169
) that
\frac{1}{F}{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}\otimes{{{{\bf P}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}^{*}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
has no branch points unless possibly in the case
\beta=\pm\frac{\bar{F}}{F}
. Taking these options for value of
\beta
, we can rewrite (
167
) as

\displaystyle F\,{\Pi}\,{\mu}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}^{\rm T}=\pm\bar{F}\,{{\bar{\mu}}}{\vphantom{\bar{\mu}}}\,\bar{{\Pi}}^{\rm T}\,.

(170)

Now we use the assumption about algebraic independence of
{{{{\bf P}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
and
{{{\bar{{\bf P}}}}}{\vphantom{\bar{{{\bf P}}}}}
to conclude that the last matrix equality cannot be satisfied.
